The question concerns the functioning of the free medicines program for seniors and is addressed to the Ministry of Health. I would like to ask Mrs Kopcinska for a question.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u4	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Dear Minister, Since 1 September 2016, there has been a government free drug programme for people aged 75+. A list with a list of medicines available free of charge is published every 2 months provided that you have a prescription with the letter S. In connection with the publication of a new list of medicines refunded from 1 May 2017, we ask the Minister with the following questions: What new medicines have been included in the list for seniors since 1 May 2017? How many people have used free meds for seniors? How many S-letter recipes were issued? How many packs of drugs have gone to Polish seniors? I also ask you to answer the question whether the Ministry is planning to extend the list of medicines that are received free of charge by seniors. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u5	Thank you very much. Please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Health of Mr Marek Tombarkiewicz.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u6	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! On the issue of medicines for seniors, medicines related to the government's 75+ programme, the last notice of 26 April 2017 of the Minister of Health greatly extended the range of medicines available free of charge for patients aged over 75 years. Three groups of medicines have been added to treat hypertension and heart failure. These are selective beta-blockers, non-selective beta-blockers and angiotensin converting enzyme inhibitors, both single-component and so-called. Combo products. Why is this group so important? That's because it's so-called. the first line of treatment for hypertension. So far, since September, the first drugs were available free of charge from the other line of treatment for hypertension. Why not one at a time? Because these drugs, which have been available so far, were medicines with 30% pay, i.e. with more pay on the part of the patient, while the drugs introduced on May 1 are medicines which have been until now with a lump sum payment, i.e. for 3.20. The fact that they were for 3,20, the patient paid so much, means that the NFZ paid significantly more to these drugs, so now, after deep analysis, we considered - this was the conclusion of both the Polish Society of Cardiology and the Polish Society of aneurysm and patient organizations - that it should be extended and give a full range of all medicines available for the treatment of hypertension and cardiovascular failure by introducing these three groups of drugs from 1 May. So far, the annual cost of patient subsidies for this group of medicines is about 140 and 150 million, which is a huge group of medicines. At the same time, I would like to remind you that all medicines on the list of these free medicines are refundables for all the reimbursement indications set out in the list of medicines. These indications are clearly described and therefore the inclusion of the drug on the list does not change the very indications in the case of registration of the product concerned. As far as the number of molecules is concerned, there are 1536 EAN codes on our list, and on September 1st we started with 1188 codes, which means that this group of drugs has increased significantly during that time. As far as implementation is concerned, around 1 million people use free medication each month. January data is 957,000. people, for February, for a shorter month - 787 thousand, for March - 942 thousand, for April - 938 thousand. persons. If we add this very large group of medicines now, we hope that over 1 million people will benefit from free medicines for seniors. As far as costs are concerned - from January was the first new list, which expanded this group of medicines, from March the second and now we have a third list - from the beginning of the year monthly costs for January amounted to 29, almost 30 million PLN, or 30 million PLN seniors did not spend their money on access to drugs, going to the pharmacy, they received drugs for which they would have to pay out of their pocket 30 million. In March this amount has already increased to 33 million, in April to almost 35 million, and now thanks to this new group of medicines this amount will grow significantly. As far as statistics are concerned, for the period from 1 September to the end of April there were 22 million packs of medicines released free of charge to patients aged 75 years and over. During this period, they used these medicines - because monthly this means that each month they do not add up, but are counted according to PESEL numbers - each month almost 1 million people use them, while adding up from the beginning of September to the end of April, there were 1727 thousand of them. We estimated that the seniors who would benefit from this would be over 2 million to almost 3 million, so we are already at the level of more than half of this entire population that has the right to benefit from it. As you can see, not everyone has the authority to do so. And when it comes to the number of prescriptions, it was 4.8 million prescriptions marked with S code, i.e. those free medicines for seniors. Since 1 January, the list has been extended to include all insulin preparations and insulin analogues included in the list of reimbursement medicines. In 2016, they have been analogues since September, but now they are human insulin. Since 1 March we have extended the list to include all medicines refunded to date in the glaucoma and the basic drug used in Parkinson's disease, i.e. levodopa, while since 1 May, as I have already said, there is a very large extension of the group of medicines against hypertension and against cardiovascular failure. As far as expansion is concerned, last year we had 125 million for 4 months, this year we have 564 300 000, therefore the increase in expenditure is significant, in the following years it will immediately be 15% each year. So we're still working on expanding this list, giving new molecules to the Agency for Medical Technology Assessment and Tarification to assess whether or not these molecules, [[♪ The bell ♪]] The ones we chose should be on that list. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u7	Thank you very much. I would ask you to ask Mr Tadeusz Dziuba an additional question.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u8	Minister, yesterday at the session of the Sejm Committee on Health, there was a debate on the availability of transplant drugs. Question: "Do patients, especially seniors, encounter difficulties in obtaining these medicines?"
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u9	Please, Minister Marek Tombarkiewicz.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u10	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! There was a heated debate on the Health Committee yesterday. I would like to remind you - because it was a bit of heating up anxiety among patients - that the 2011 Refund Act, which came into force in 2012, assumes a very specific, rigidly described mechanism for determining the basis of the limit for a given drug. And that's what happens every two months. I would like to remind you that in 2012 and 2015 there were 16 such very loud cases, when on the next new list a given drug, which was previously for a lump sum, i.e. for 3,20, suddenly became a medicine with a payment of several hundred zlotys or more than a thousand. That's what happened on the May list now. Medicines that are used in transplant patients, i.e. immunosuppressants, two original medicines, which have so far been with a lump sum payment for 3.20, because there have been equivalents, i.e. medicines with the same bioequivalence. I want to place particular emphasis on the fact that all the drugs we are introducing are medicines with proven efficacy, strength and safety. All this is in the documentation of the registration office of medicinal products. There's absolutely no contraindications to convert the original drug into a replacement drug. In the case of these medicines, a medicine that has become the basis of the limit, i.e. a substitute, is available for a flat-rate payment, i.e. 3.20. However, when it comes to the original drug, unfortunately this payment for patients has increased significantly, but this is a normal mechanism of the reimbursement bill. Thanks to this, the savings that can be made are spent on purchasing further new therapies for many patients in need. We still have a problem with the availability of modern anti-cancer drugs in Poland and all the savings that we obtain thanks to risk sharing instruments, thanks to very difficult negotiations with pharmaceutical companies, thanks to such a structure of the reimbursement bill... There are certainly some inconveniences here, but the very mechanism of switching the original drug to a substitute medicine, which is much cheaper - and thus subsidies from the state budget are significantly less - generates savings that we can use to buy new therapies. And in the context of directly seniors, as I say, drugs that are registered in the same indications... The replacement medicine and the original medicine have the same indications. As far as all the documentation is concerned, at European level there is an EMA, i.e. the European Medicines Agency, and in Poland there is an office for registration of medicinal products. Registration of the medicine, thus allowing the refund to be placed on the market at a later stage, is a very strict process and all of these medicines are medicines of the same effectiveness and safety. [[♪ The bell ♪]] So that there is absolutely no risk here when it comes to the use of drugs, about drugs replacement versus original medicines, for patients. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u15	Thank you very much. Please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Development, Mrs Jadwiga Emilewicz.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u16	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Thank you for asking us about the development of the programme relating to the railway industry in Poland. That's two important threads, I think. One is that this industry is indeed present on the Polish market and is actually ready to deliver all products that run on rails in the world, maybe except for high-speed railways. It is also worth mentioning that today it is 11 thousand. jobs, and the revenue of this sector last year was PLN 8 billion. So that this is a very important branch of the economy. The Ministry of Development sees this and realizes it. Therefore, among others. The "Luxtorpeda' programme as one of the flagship programmes has been entered into the "Strategy for Responsible Development' programme. How is the implementation of this programme materialized? First of all, it's based on three dimensions. The first is the development of innovative products, and therefore the development of R &amp; D, which will aim to develop new generation products based on the industrial and R &amp; D potential present in Poland. The second important topic is the purchase of new goods, new vehicles by railway transport companies present on the Polish market. And the third thread, also very important, is the modernisation of the rolling stock. We are aware that rail transport, rail transport is the blood of the state economy, as well as that rail transport has a very strong emphasis on European policies. At the same time, we know about the average age of rolling stock in Poland. It's 30 years, all the railway companies are saying what it looks like today. Thus, on the one hand, the modernisation of existing rolling stock and, on the other hand, the purchase of new products is not only a choice but also a necessity. The key effect, the aim we have set ourselves in implementing the "Luxtorpeda" programme, is to create competitive vehicles and therefore vehicles that will not only be present on our market but also be our export goods. We are aware that increasing this competitiveness can actually only occur if new projects, new passenger wagons, locomotives or integrated vehicles are developed, which, as I say, will both meet the needs of our market and can be offered outside. This concerns measures and the implementation of the entire project. One of them, especially from the R &amp; D area, and this was the second question, is two threads. One is the Innotabortion programme. This is a sector programme developed in cooperation with companies operating on the Polish market, on the other hand with R &amp; D centres dealing, in a broad sense, with rail transport projects. This is a project for which the total allocation is PLN 1.2 billion. In the first competition closed at the beginning of this year, applications were selected for a total amount of PLN 160 million. Contracts will be signed at the beginning of the third quarter of this year. These projects submitted in the framework of this competition concern both urban rail transport and cross-urban rail transport. Using the results of these projects will allow serial production. However, there is also a second very important thread in the implementation of these R &amp; D projects, which at the moment we are completing in cooperation in the new mode adopted last year. Almost public procurement, which allows the performance of the contract in so-called. the innovation partnership procedure. For this partnership, we need one who finally orders a product that is not yet on the market today. Therefore, the process of reaching this order is very complicated. But if we are talking about innovation, these major players must be innovative. That is why we have developed together with companies that provide rail transport services in Poland, the extent to which they are interested in the following areas: locomotive, wagon, integrated transport, which products are necessary for them and which they are looking for, which is not on the market today. At the moment we are finishing to define the framework of this competition again within the National Centre for Research and Development. It's gonna be a contest. While Innotabor is an open innovation, an inquiry to research teams, which is interesting to suggest, is a tailor-made product, i.e. companies know what they need, what they lack, and declare that if, as a result of research and development, a project to which a pilot product will be produced meets all technical and price expectations, they will declare the purchase of an appropriate number of the product concerned. So that is also a very innovative way of ordering. We assume that this competition will be announced in the third quarter. We know that it will take 30 months of such highly disciplined work to complete such a new product with certification with tests. So after 30 months we can hope that these new products will physically run on tracks or rails in Poland. [[♪ The bell ♪]] So that we predict that significant modernisation of transport, these new products between 2021 and 2026 will enter the market. As far as I'm concerned... Looks like only half a minute...

At the moment these losses are valued, this is the information from yesterday, at PLN 46 million. It's a loss of more than 70%. in a given crop, and it should be noted that earlier, i.e. by 2015, government aid was directed to those farmers who were 70% losses throughout the holding. We have changed this to 70% in a given crop so that this aid is more open, more accessible to those farmers who are suffering losses due to negative weather phenomena. Such an amendment of the Regulation entered into force in 2016. This aid will be addressed to those farmers who had such losses in 2017. Of course, the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture has a current agreement on preferential loans with eight banks. All information on the possibility of receiving these loans can be found, among others. on the website of the restructuring agency. Such assistance shall be granted each year, the President of the Agency shall sign such an agreement. The President of the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund, at the individual request of the farmer who has just suffered losses on the farm, can spread out on instalments, may extend the contribution, may also cancel the contribution. The President of the Agricultural Property Agency on the basis of the Act on the Management of Agricultural Real Estate of the State Treasury may defer or extend payments for sales contracts or lease of land from the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury into instalments. It also depends on the good will of local government officials, because under the Act Tax Order local government officials, i.e. the mayors, mayors or presidents of the cities where the agricultural land is located, can postpone, extend or cancel the so-called. agricultural tax. In addition, as I mentioned, in response to Mr Maliszewski's question, I assure you that if these losses are already valued, the committees will complete their work and the protocols will flow to us, we will, on the basis of the valuation of these losses, direct the aid to all those farmers who have suffered losses of 70%. Surely such support for these farmers will be addressed. It should also be remembered that negative weather phenomena did not end in April, did not end in May, because it is still June and July. There could be hail, there could be hurricanes, strong winds, heavy rains. As regards those risks, which are very heavily outlined in the 2005 Act, farmers still have the possibility to cover these risks. For the first time in the state budget there is almost PLN 1 billion of support for these farmers. We add 65% to every risk. , not 50% , as before, so that this aid is adequate to primarily promote this aid and the universality of agricultural insurance. If farmers are not insured, no aid addressed to them will be able to offset or cover the losses incurred in specific crops. We are aware of the losses incurred mainly by the gardeners and gardeners, as well as the losses in plant production, but above all the most urgent is the valuation of these losses. Only then will concrete government assistance be addressed.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u113	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you for asking. I am pleased to provide you with information on the increase in budget revenue from the various tax sources of the state budget. Before I present the dry numbers, I'd like to give them a little bit of an indication of where it came from. According to us, the increase in revenue, tax, is due to the clear determination of the government, the Ministry of Finance in particular, in reducing the pathology of the tax system, the theoretical tax system that we have been dealing with for a long time. We show initiative, commitment and efficiency both on the legislative side and by adopting modern or proven international solutions, we demonstrate operational efficiency through the new formula of the National Tax Administration, but we also try to inform taxpayers and warn them against entering the pathologies that are taking place in our tax system. We are too late for the countries of our region by about 4 and 5 years. The Czech Republic and Slovakia, which I have recently had the opportunity to consult with, are four to five years ahead of all the solutions that we are introducing very intensively today, with full commitment, but these delayed actions, when you look at the whole state, have caused the situation that we need to unearth ourselves from a much worse tax hole than they do, because their sealing actions have caused the migration of pathologies to Poland as a jurisdiction that is easy to extort, for example, VAT. We are therefore carrying out holistic measures on all taxes. It is difficult to talk about the reasons for CIT or PIT sealing in isolation from VAT. We see cross-effects in terms of the fact that, even if the VAT JPK creates more discipline on the part of income taxes, greater discipline on the side of corporate income taxes causes more money to be paid to employees in official form, resulting in an increase in PIT income, and thus holistic measures have a systemic dimension and lead to an increase in budgetary revenue. This is no surprise, because the tax systems of the countries are not designed by chance, they are constructed just so that one tax looks at the other and the whole system is supposed to work smoothly. That's why we holistically perceive this problem and move forward. In some respects, we are chasing the neighbouring or Western countries. This is the case with the JPK VAT basic instrument, which is submitted electronically by entrepreneurs for the time being the largest, ultimately all. We are chasing the countries of our region in the field of online fiscal cash registers, which cause phenomenal sealing in our neighbours in the south. These are modern technologies that these countries have implemented or implemented. Unfortunately, it is only now that we have to make up for this delay. There are some layers of our actions that are completely ahead of what is happening in Europe. I mean the split payment mechanism according to this Polish idea based on banking systems and a separate class of accounts in which VAT can move safely for entrepreneurs, ensuring both the safety of trading for ordinary entrepreneurs and the safety of public finances. The system is intended to prevent what has been happening for many years in connection with the export of VAT due abroad. In total, Poland is deprived of any instruments that could bring this VAT back to Poland. We also have proposals for so-called. aggressive tax optimization. Here we are again operating on many levels. Firstly, we inform taxpayers so as to give them an opportunity, an opportunity to correct their accounts when we are clearly dealing with fictional structures that simply allow them not to pay income tax, and thus these companies, once, abuse, so to speak, trust in the fiscal, and, on the other hand, unfairly compete with the competition that pays this tax. We are trying to create a secure ecosystem for businesses, safe and fair as regards tax burdens. We are introducing whether we have a legislative proposal ready to disclose these mechanisms of aggressive tax optimization. Commissioner Moscovici recently proposed to start this type of work at European Union level. We are advanced in the process of preparing these solutions and I hope that next week the project will be made public. This also aims to align the playing field for small businesses that do not have the possibility to optimize international taxes like market leaders. These are actions that I think are ahead of... This split payment mechanism, the disclosure mechanism of aggressive tax optimization, these mechanisms are things that allow us a little bit to overtake the jurisdiction of our region in terms of building safe space, fair for entrepreneurs. Interestingly enough, and we also talked about this with international organisations, it turns out that our sealing activities, at least some of them, meet with a lot of support from industry organisations. The number of pathologies in the system so far was such that it was difficult to conduct business honestly, and industry organisations even support us in the rapid implementation of these improvements, which, I say, we welcome. The cost of these seals is certainly not on the side of economic growth, because this one is doing well. Therefore, before I move on to dry figures, I would like to ask Parliament's sympathy to proceed with our subsequent legislative proposals and those already in the legislative process. And the dry facts are clear and quite boring, if I say so,: VAT revenue in January April 2016 increased by 34%, i.e. about PLN 14.5 billion, excise tax revenues higher by 3.6 years per year, i.e. about 0.7 billion, PIT revenues higher by 7.6% year-to-year, i.e. by 1.1 billion, and CIT revenues higher by 15% year to year, or about 1.7 billion. Thank you. [[Applause]] [[President of the session includes Deputy Marshal Barbara Dolniak Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u198	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Today I have the honour to present a draft Water Law Act, which is a government project with among others. for the implementation of European Union law. As we read in the introduction to the Sejm's website, the project concerns, among others. the establishment of a new legal entity of the State legal entity of the Polish Waters, whose task will be to finance investments in water management, as well as the efficient acquisition of funds for these investments, including non-budget funds, as well as the regulation of water management issues, including the power over assigned water management units, carried out on behalf of the State Treasury. Briefly about the story. I have here a water law from 1922, the first law in the independent Republic of Poland, which, you can say, was very modern for those times and many of these provisions are in the present draft law. This law was in force until 1962, the next new Water Law in the Polish People's Republic was in 1974 and only in 2001 was the first Water Law in the Third Republic. However, it did not cover all the issues related to the state's systemic reform, so there was a lack of funding system, transparent financial instruments, and there was no integration of water management competences. In 2007, we tried to prepare a new Water Law. Unfortunately, because of earlier elections, it was not possible to do so. Between 2008 and 2015, the PO-PSL coalition attempted to proceed with the Water Law Act, but in 2015, before the elections, it was interrupted for fear of causing social unrest. At the same time, there have been repeated amendments related to EU law. The Community water policy, as provided for in the Water Framework Directive, requires a transparent, efficient and coherent legislative framework, including in the national legal order. The new Water Law therefore aims to introduce instruments to ensure that the Water Framework Directive, such as the Water Framework Directive, is achieved. the full implementation of a water-based water management policy that meets the sustainability criteria. The concept of water catchment should be understood to mean that this implementation takes place not within the administrative limits, but within the natural, hydrographic limits, i.e. in areas from which water flows into a single river. What is meant by sustainable development? It is a rapid economic growth, increasing the wealth of Poles and Poles while respecting the principles governing nature, for present and future generations. Water is one of the most important factors in the success of sustainable development. The fact that water is life is a certain truism, but it is a truism as real as possible. Ensuring good water for residents, providing water for various economic purposes, agriculture, energy, inland waterway transport, industry, finally ensuring the availability of water for rest and recreation, as well as for biodiversity, for all aquatic and terrestrial organisms - all this is a challenge for the state. At the same time, it is necessary to ensure the safety of Poles and Poles, protection against extreme phenomena such as flooding, drought or other extraordinary environmental threats. All these tasks must be the responsibility of the state. Water resources shall not be subject to any privatisation process. That's what I wanted to emphasize very strongly. Water management is not an area in itself, but it must play a service role for all users, very often reconcile different, conflicting interests. In order to achieve all this, it is necessary to organise it in such a way as to ensure its efficient, long-term and systematic management. Of course, adequate funding is needed for this, not on the basis of action following floods and droughts, but on the basis of anticipation and countermeasures. The lack or excess of water leads to conflicts at local, national and international level, which is an example, among others. The war in Syria. Water is expected to be either a growth barrier or a rapid growth. Poland is a very poor country in water. The water resources in Poland amount to around 1500 to 1600 m3 per capita and are comparable to Egypt's water resources. We are practically the last place in continental Europe in this area. The new Water Law also means ensuring real and effective water power understood in accordance with Article. 3 the Water Framework Directive and its adequate impact on water management issues, with particular emphasis on strategic planning and the conservation of water resources. The water authority shall determine the directions of investment in the waters, ensure the maintenance of water equipment and ensure that good water status is achieved. Finally, water management manages the flood risk and ensures that it is minimised at all levels of water management. One of the most important issues that are very urgent is the fulfilment of ex ante conditionality, i.e. the preconditions for the use of European funds. Poland was obliged to comply with these ex ante conditions by the end of 2016. This includes: Water laws and water management plans. As far as water law is concerned, this is a full implementation of the Water Framework Directive, and in particular Article. 9 on the reimbursement of the costs of water services, in other words, towards self-financing of water management, as is already the case for water and sewage undertakings. The reimbursement of water services costs is simply a balancing of revenue and expenditure. To this end, a new water financing system is introduced based on flexible financial instruments which should ensure efficient and efficient management of water resources and the allocation of resources to actions to ensure the availability of water with adequate quality and quantity parameters, protection against flooding and drought and all other objectives to be pursued by water management. What does Water Law include? Water law covers the implementation of as many as eight EU directives, including, of course, the Water Framework Directive, the Flood Directive, the Urban Waste Water Treatment Directive, the Bathing Directive, the Groundwater Quality Directive, and changes nearly 50 laws in its scope. The project contains 570 articles and has 401 pages. And now, briefly - in a telegraph context - I will discuss the contents of this law. The following chapters and provisions are included. The general provisions contain definitions of water and water bodies, in other words, they regulate the division into hydrographic boundaries, the use of water and water services. I would like to say that these regulations, which were contained in the Water Law, are maintained, divided into three basic types of water use: ordinary - water collection for the farm's own needs of 5 m3 per day; universal - the right to general access to water flowing, i.e. to bathing, to sailing by tourist boats, as was the case in Prewar Law, even slipping in the winter, if there is ice, in other words, to rest, which is extremely important for our countrymen who use water in the summer, in this case the principle is maintained, we cannot restrict access to water; special - the use of water on the basis of water law permits, i.e. if someone wants to take water for industrial, agricultural purposes, to undertake various kinds of water-related activities, then he must obtain water law. So the use of water is divided into ordinary, common and special. The next chapter includes the implementation of the European Union Water Baths Directive. I am aware that there will be some comments here, because indeed the creation of a bathing area by the municipality is extremely complicated and difficult, but these are the regulations of the EU directive that we need to take into account. The Water Law then provides for the protection of waters taking into account environmental objectives, lays down rules for the protection of waters against pollution, concerning the treatment of municipal waste water. In other words, the national urban waste water treatment programme is also the result of the implementation of the EU directive with regard to the so-called. agglomerations of more than 2 000. residents. Here we have introduced some new rules for the approval of the agglomeration. No longer will the marshals approve, but they will approve the commune and the Polish Water farm, because our experience shows that the marshals were delayed with the approval of the agglomeration, and consequently the consequences were that the municipality which was not included in the National Urban Waste Water Treatment Program could not obtain funds for its activities. That's why we simplified it. Another extremely important issue is the protection of water against pollution of nitrates from agricultural sources. Poland had an infringement in 2012, and a judgment was taken in 2014. Here we change the entire water protection system against nitrate pollution from agricultural sources. Namely, so far, the so-called ones have been designated. OSN, i.e. areas particularly vulnerable to pollution with these nitrates. There were often large farms in these areas, large farms that actually contributed to nitrate pollution, but at the same time small farms that did not cause pollution, and farmers had to bear the consequences of having to carry out certain equipment and spend quite large amounts on it. In the project presented, we propose to introduce a single action programme for the whole country, but taking into account the size of agricultural holdings, so farms with an area of up to 10 hectares and up to 10 large conversion units will not have to carry out special equipment, however in good agricultural practices this will be recommended. I'd like to say that we already have a conviction for violation. June 23rd will be the so-called. package meeting and unfortunately threaten us with penalties, very high penalties - either a one-off penalty of a dozen or tens of millions of euros, or daily penalties of several thousand euros per day. So that this is an extremely important issue, the implementation of this directive. The next chapter includes pollutants, i.e. mainly so-called. Priority. Thanks to the parliamentary initiative, this matter has already been taken into account. I wanted to thank you for that. The next chapter includes the protection of water shots and inland water reservoirs, which is an extremely important thing, about water intended for human consumption. This is more restrictive than until now, namely the obligation to protect the direct zone and to carry out a risk analysis for the intermediate zone. This means that whoever takes water for supply will have to do this risk analysis with regard to impurities. If he doesn't, they'll do it to Polish Waters and then charge the cost. We must take great care of the quality of water. We have more and more cases related to poor water quality. However, in many cities, especially in large cities, this quality is improving, there are increasingly different types of water pollution, due to, for example, drought and reduction of water resources, and hence, concentration of pollutants. The next chapter concerns the protection of the environment of marine waters, i.e. marine waters, and this draft law deals with the environment of marine waters. The next chapter concerns flood risk management. Here we have proposed new regulations. This is what happened before the 2015 novel. The municipalities had no right to build, no development of areas in areas covered by flood risk maps, which caused, often with wrong maps, that simply could not follow the development of cities. We are now introducing a different mechanism, namely to agree on a spatial planning plan by the Polish Waters, that is, on the basis of maps we will determine whether it is really possible to build in this area, whether it is not possible to build or whether it is possible to build with certain limitations. The next very important chapter includes combating the effects of drought. Recent years have shown that this is a growing problem. The next chapters concern water construction and water melioration. As far as water meliorations are concerned, we're changing the definition. There is no longer any division into water meliorations basic and detailed, simply water meliorations are detailed meliorations, i.e., in other words, ditches, mainly ditches and other small devices, while all other, flood shafts, which were previously primary water melioration devices, are water devices. Polish waters will keep records of detailed meliorations in the IT system. The next important department includes the management of the State Treasury property, water ownership and the duties of its owners. Ownership rights of the Treasury in relation to flowing waters owned by the State Treasury will be exercised by the Polish Waters - in relation to inland flowing waters and groundwater. Underground waters are associated with flowing waters, mostly quaternary pieces, and I wanted to say as a curiosity that powering Polish rivers, rivers in Poland, that is about equally, half of groundwater comes from underground waters, and half directly from precipitation. However, at the request of the Minister for Maritime Affairs and Inland Water Management, inland waterways of particular transport importance shall be excluded from this management by the Polish Waters. The next body to exercise ownership rights is the Minister responsible for maritime affairs - in relation to waters of the territorial sea and marine internal waters. The solutions that have been adopted so far, i.e. water management by the national park manager, have been removed from the general system. On the other hand, it is possible to make agreements with the State Forests and with local government units, in other words, if there are any leaks in the park, the park will be able to carry out its tasks as it has so far. The same is true when it comes to State Forests, as it is now, also on the basis of agreements, but there is also a novelty that with local government units. If cities want to manage smaller courses - and there are such cities, among others. Gdansk, Warsaw - they will simply be able to do so on the basis of agreements. It will also be possible to conclude agreements at each level of local government, i.e. municipal and voivodship. And here the most important change: the establishment of the National Water Farm of Poland - this is a state legal person - as an entity responsible for water management throughout the country. The National Water Farm of Poland, contrary to what is often said, will not be one large centralized unit, but will be a hierarchical unit. From the bottom it will be water surveillance, then water catchment management - these water catchment management boards will be about 50 - regional water management management boards and the National Water Management Board. Most of the competences are so distributed in the Water Law Act that they really are as decentralised as possible. We were wondering if we could set up separate boards, as it was in the draft PO-PSL Act, that the former Vistula River Management Board and the Oder River Management Board, but due to uneven revenues from individual areas, all needs will have to be evened out. It could be like that. in Silesia, where there are very high income, there would be an excess of money, for example. in areas of Eastern Poland, where there are small revenues and large needs, there would be a shortage of these resources. This is a mechanism that will regulate spending. This describes the financial economy of Polish Waters. The revenue will be the fees that I will continue to talk about, and will include other rental income, as well as national budgetary resources and EU funds. As regards the management of the State Treasury's property, we have here a division, i.e. the Polish Waters and the Minister responsible for inland waterway transport, with regard to inland waterways of particular transport importance. We're still gonna keep talking. Mostly it's about Oder and the shipping facilities in Oder. We're introducing economic instruments. This is of course the most controversial, but this is what the Water Framework Directive requires - the reimbursement of the costs of water services. So far these fees, this toll system has been in the Environmental Law. I would like to remind you that in the current law, the maximum rate for the collection of water in this almost amounts to 4 PLN. We call this under the Water Framework Directive water service charges. These are charges such as the collection of groundwater or surface water, the introduction of sewage into water or to land, the discharge of waste water from sewage systems, the drainage of land within the administrative limits of cities, the collection of groundwater and surface water for fish farming and farming, and the introduction into water or land of waste water from such fish farming or farming. We are introducing new mechanisms to ensure that we do not deprive ourselves of natural terrain retention. Therefore, in the case of built-up properties with an area of more than 3,5 thousand. m2 built in 70% permanent, sealed surface property owners will bear charges which will decrease if compensatory measures are carried out, such as for example. water tanks or other water retention systems. The next fee is related to the extraction of stone, gravel, sand and other materials from surface waters. It is also a question of equalizing, however, side-activity, mainly for regulation, the cost of fees from geological concessions with those from Water Law. As for who will determine these fees, they will determine them by decision of the Polish Waters. Any water intake and waste water discharge will be measured by the Polish Waters. So far, the system has relied on voluntary declarations by users. Because this was the system, we do not really know exactly how much water is being collected in Poland, because many companies, companies simply lowered these values from the declaration, and there was not an appropriate control system that would show how much this water is actually collected. Another extremely important function of Water Law is planning. It is about planning that has been launched in the previous Water Law, but is currently covered by EU directives. These are river basin management plans related to these water management plans flood risk management plans, water-environment plans, drought prevention plans. The Minister responsible for Water Management is also drawing up a plan to protect the marine environment. We will introduce an IT system for water management in place of the so-called. water cadastre. This watercaster is also a historical name, but because it is more associated with another cataster, it has been changed to an IT system of water management. This system's a little operational right now. The main goal is to balance all water management, all water resources. We need to know exactly how, where, who and how much water can be taken. I also wanted to return to the fact that, in addition to these variable fees, we introduce fixed fees. These fixed fees are aimed at balancing water resources. The point is, if someone takes, let's assume, more water from the river than any percentage specified in the bill, this fixed fee will increase. We want to ensure the amount of water in the river, availability for other users, and we also want to rationalise these activities, because with water permits someone needed e.g. 1 m3 /s, but just in case he applied for a permit of 10 m3 /s, which blocked other users from using water. Here, by the way, I would like to point out the media information that due to fixed charges the price of water for consumers will increase by PLN 30.40. These fees are at a low level so far, and they're gonna... This is an increase of about 20 gr per year for a 4-person family, or 5 gr per capita. Another extremely important issue is water management control. Since this check was actually written down, but it did not function well, we have clarified some of the procedures involved. Water monitoring is equally important. He's run by the Environmental Protection Inspection. This water monitoring will be used to know what the state of the waters is and then to be able to counteract. The next chapter concerns water power. This concept of water power is precisely from this law of 1922. Here, the Minister responsible for water management will play a key role, who will have supervision over subordinate entities. The National Council for Water Management, the advisory and advisory body of the Minister responsible for water management, will also be set up. Another extremely important thing that is already in operation, but which we want to improve, is the state services: the state hydrological and meteorological service, the state hydrogeological service and the security service for building constructions - this is a service that has been in operation for some time; in fact, before the war, a global organization was created, called the Polish Security Committee for High Dams. All these services will also be financed by means of Polish Waters. The next element is water law. Why do we call it water law? Because at the moment there was only the concept of water law permit. Here we distinguish several types of this water law consent. The first type of water law consent is so-called. Waterclub report. That's thanks, too. I would like to thank you Members for taking this into account, and we introduced this already in 2015. Now we're expanding this directory a little bit. For small water investments of various kinds, for small enterprises, such as the construction of a bridge, the construction of a small pond or a bathing facility, there will be no need for water law permits, only a notification, very simplified, complex lowest - where you live - means in water surveillance. If, after 30 days, there will be no objection, as will the construction reports, it means that it already works. Of course, water law permits remain, water law decisions for all more complex projects. Here, too, I wanted to talk about a system, about a change. Namely the competent authority will be the Polish Waters. These competences will be transferred from old age, from marshals to Polish Waters. Also, as has been done so far, what the old men have done will be on the management board of the water catchment area. What the marshals did will be on the regional water management boards. Any application for water law will be filed in the nearest water surveillance area where someone will be found. Already the issue of Polish Waters will be where this proposal is actually directed on the ground. I also wanted to say here - because I missed a thing, I'm very sorry - about this uniformity in water management management. By virtue of the law, government tasks in the local government administration concerning the current water melioration of basic water, i.e. flood shafts will be performed by the Polish Waters. So the employees from the management board will go to the Polish Waters. There is no need to transfer shafts to Polish Waters, because these devices are devices of the State Treasury. At the moment there is such a system that the budget through the voivodeship transfers funds to the Marshal to carry out tasks and marshals through the voivodship management boards of water equipment melioration of these tasks. These water permits will be issued by the Polish Waters, but only to external entities, because they cannot issue water permits themselves. Therefore, Polish Waters will be granted water law permits by the Minister responsible for water management. We are also introducing a semi-automatic mechanism for extending water law permits, i.e. reducing red tape. The water law permit shall be issued for a limited period of time. If there is no change, it is enough to apply for an extension. The next novel concerns the introduction of water law assessment. The purpose of the water law assessment is to determine whether the investment concerned is in breach of good water status. In other words, it is an assessment from the 47th derogation of the Water Framework Directive whether this investment is in breach of the Water Framework Directive. If there is a deterioration in water status, it can be done under certain conditions, which is why we introduced it. It is also true that it is not an easy project, it is probably best known by Minister Gawłowski, who worked on Water Law for several years. We therefore have no objection to the government's presentation of its author's proposal, but we cannot agree that, in addition to the necessary and urgent implementation of the directive establishing a framework for Community action in the field of water policy, the aim of this law is to centralise the performance of water management tasks. Is that a big surprise? No. Centralisation and the taking away of tasks that have been successfully carrying them out over the years are a permanent element of every legislative process of the Government of Law and Justice. The draft law is therefore another link in the chain of legislative changes centralising everything that is possible because the government does not recognise the principle of decentralisation of public tasks and finances. Contrary to the millions of Poles and Poles who appreciate the important role played by the government, the PiS state, instead of adding powers to local governments, wants to take them back. And that's the first important thread I'd like to focus on for a while. All water management tasks carried out by the Old Towns, Marshal Offices and their units, mainly the provincial management of water drainage and equipment, are taken over by the State Water Farm of Poland. You are proposing such revolutionary solutions without a reliable analysis of whether your proposal makes sense. I have 15 years of experience working in the local government and I remember very well what is the difference - even in the case of processing and issuing water permits - in terms of the performance of tasks by government and local government administration. It's about processing time, the quality of service. There is no comparison, of course, in favour of the local government. I have no doubt that there won't be much time, and you'll find out for yourself. The second task is to improve flood safety. It is true that this task is commissioned, that it is largely financed by the state budget, even from the reserve that you have radically cut for the past two years. But without organisational and financial involvement, flood safety would be at a much lower level. The funds obtained from European funds, from the World Bank, environmental funds and own budgets are counted in tens of billions of zlotys. I don't want it to sound like I don't see the need for change. I think it's right to put in place a water catchment system, a combination of the river manager and a downfall along the river. This rational action should have been introduced long ago. It will be easier to manage, plan and implement investments. Today, too, I must tell you that I understand better the motives behind the taking away of environmental funds from the regions. It's quiet tonight. Nobody protests that 35% of the fees charged by the Marshals of the voivodships will go directly to the Polish Voivodship. Environmental funds will practically remain without funding, but they will have the tasks to finance them. Entrepreneurs and local governments will find it much harder to raise funds for the fight for clean air, for sewerage, for a number of other programs. I don't understand why you want to turn on little leaks in the Polish Waters. After all, no one from the central level will be doing this and, I believe, on the basis of the projection of the Polish Waters budget, will have the resources for this task. The government in a wide range of financial revenues of Polish Vodka wrote down subsidies from local government units, while in the Memorandum of Understanding after the adoption of this project it noted that the Council of Ministers in relation to the planned creation of PGW Polskie Water does not assume additional funding from the state budget. So the myth that money would flow from the state budget ended its existence on October 18th. Why? The answer is simple. It will probably not take long to find out in practice. I want to tell you that this was the case, but then it didn't work out. I think that is also the motive that you have not agreed on this project in the Joint Government and Territorial Government Commission. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u220	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party, I would like to present the position of the club on the draft Water Law Act, print no. 1529. The draft law submitted is an attempt to adapt Polish law to the requirements of European standards contained in the Water Framework Directive. The aim of this regulation should be to create a transparent, efficient and coherent legislative framework. Such regulation would be guaranteed by the establishment of a real and effective water authority with an impact on water management planning, water protection, including investment in water, maintenance of water facilities and ultimately maintaining good water status. That's the idea. What will end with an attempt to introduce the proposed provisions? One thing is certain: this trial will end in total chaos. Already now, the resort of the environment with the Ministry of Maritime Economy is competing for water management, and the Minister of Energy also sees in geothermal water rescue, an opportunity to improve the Polish energy balance. The idea of creating an effective power includes the establishment of the State Water Farm of Poland - a megastate legal person. Similarity to the State Forest Forest Farm seems not to be accidental and even uniforming appears, only that State Forests are an institution with almost an age-old tradition, and Polish Waters are simply a political idea for consolidation of many institutions, including the acquisition of voivodship management boards which are currently part of the administration of the voivodship government. Minister, in this connection, I have a question: What about the staff of the melioration boards, the experts, the long-term experts? Will they, as in the Civil Service Act, lose their day-to-day work for the more disposable ones? The Polish waters are to be the property management unit of the State Treasury, as well as the water authority itself, to be responsible for the acquisition and collection of funds generated by water management, as well as their spending on investments in water management and the maintenance of water and other property of the State Treasury. Polish waters will be a centralised unit of government administration with an extensive, multi-level internal structure. How is this machine supposed to find a citizen who wants to build a pier on a lake or a farmer who has problems with the drainage of his land? I have serious concerns that the proposed changes will not make life easier for the citizen. A significant part of the proposed law is provisions on different types of water service charges, including the setting of a maximum of those charges. It appears that such solutions may require frequent amendment of the law and should be referred back to specific provisions in the form of a regulation. It should be noted that the government is eager to point out the necessary elimination of the existing exemptions for the use of water resources. You say: it's EU rules, you have to pay so that we can meet the ex ante conditions, which will allow us to mobilise aid for water and sewage investments. However, in the European Union, firstly, it is possible to adapt the rates to the economic opportunities of the Member States, and secondly, nowhere is it indicated that the citizen and his wallet should be responsible for covering the costs of this obligation. It is the citizen who will bear the costs of several hundred percent increase in the costs and charges associated with the collection of drinking water and the discharge of melting and rainwater, which local authorities will ultimately cover. The potential impact on entrepreneurs is also alarming. The amount of fees and the uncertainty about the timing of their introduction and the way in which they are calculated, the variable annual rates, the obligation to purchase meters for each beneficiary cause great concern to farmers, fishermen, beverage producers and other food producers that the fees will significantly reduce their ability to compete with producers from other countries where there are no or they are illusory. The introduction of the law will also lead to a revolution in the health finance system. The Polish Waters will receive part of the fees that went to environmental and water funds and voivodship funds. It is estimated that the income of the voivodship funds may fall by up to 50%. Is this not a threat to the continuity of funding and the functioning of these funds? Are there any plans to change the name of these funds and the extent to which they operate? The new law also provides for the creation of a price regulator. It would start operating from 2019 as part of the new Polish Water Institute. The price regulator will enter the local authorities' existing competences, specifying the price of water and waste water. It seems that this is just a step towards taking over local government institutions and water and sewage companies. I would also like to emphasise the regional theme, because Warmia and Mazury, from which I come, are associated with water. Tourists, hotelers, sailors express great concern about the tolls for piers and piers on lakes. Similar concerns arise from the use of fishing waters. Is this supposed to be a fishing model, or is it supposed to be an economic model? The issue of fish in the lakes of Warmia and Mazury is an extremely important and crazy matter, strongly affecting the development of the tourism of my region. Until now, management has been scattered. Some waters were subject to local governments and some to central authorities. There is no discussion that this has hindered coordination of actions, particularly during the threat. The draft Water Law Act also provides for the creation of about 50 water catchment managements throughout Poland. Full implementation of water management policies that meet functional and safety criteria is an important task that we face in the context not only of economic but also of society. The draft law aims to implement more than 20 EU directives and ensure the sustainable management of water resources in accordance with the existing European Union directives in Poland, including the Strategic Adaptation Plan for Climate Change-sensitive Areas and Sectors. The introduction of a new water law will promote the sustainable development of Poland and stabilise the situation of this area of the economy. According to the Act, the State Water Farm of Poland will have the power to raise extra-budget funds while fully controlling its activities and investments. The creation of a new water unit in Poland will relieve local governments, which will have fewer obligations resulting from the tasks commissioned by the government administration. As for the regulator, we are actually working on this and, as one of the ladies or Members has said, these prices should often simply be reduced. [[Applause]] Will this result in a reduction in the competence of municipal, district and voivodship authorities? I wanted to remind you one thing that these local governments were doing government tasks, just as the government's job is to issue water law permits, just as it was the government's job to keep equipment, so-called. basic meliorations, i.e. generally shafts. The Member was worried about cycling paths here. The Marshals will continue to be able to build and use bike paths. It is not that if it is owned by the Treasury, it cannot be used. Each citizen can use by agreement and the same local authorities by agreement with the Polish Waters will be able to manage in their waters. It's not like that at all. Back to that Water Law, there were two water basin managements: Vistula and Oder. It's a beautiful thing, but it only covered the main rivers, and all the others to the local governments, to the poor, and let them keep it... If you want to see local governments that they're going to keep all this stuff without money at their own expense, then go ahead, but I know you don't want it. Next question on the fees: Will the villagers pay the rain levy for each roof and yard? No, it will be only charges for large storage sites, parking lots at hypermarkets with an area of more than 3.5 thousand. m? Next question: Will the new law not limit the development of inland waterways? It will not limit, and it is not, as Mr Gawłowski said, that this is not in line with the Water Framework Directive. Water roads on rivers can exist, just as there can be a water power plant, private, energy companies. The firewall in Solin is owned by energy companies. All of these entities can only function, as the Water Framework Directive says, they cannot issue water law permits. Neither the energy company nor shipping will issue water permits, it simply uses part of the water. Why are underground waters subject to the Water Law Act, not the Geological and Mining Law Act? This is the requirement of the Water Framework Directive, but this is also a logic, the logic of which is that groundwater and surface water are linked. I mentioned earlier that about half of the water flowing in the rivers came from groundwater and about half directly from precipitation. How will cooperation between Polish Vodka and local governments, national parks, forests look like? I already told you, they're just gonna sign agreements. How can we reconcile the implementation of the Water Framework Directive, which sets environmental objectives, with the development of the country and are there appropriate provisions? This is, ladies and gentlemen, a very big problem. The Water Framework Directive itself was established in the countries of the old Union when most waters were already degraded. We have a different situation, we are developing, and that is why we have adopted certain mechanisms that will allow us to invest at the same time in order to achieve environmental goals. However, this is one possibility and does not guarantee that it will be applied by breeders. The Polish Seed Chamber draws attention to the use and very high costs incurred by breeders in the research work on new varieties, which do not always end in obtaining varieties with planned parameters. The reimbursement of inputs and further studies are possible through the collection of licence fees and charges on own reproductions. Other risks are the lack of supervision of seed, which can lead to the spread of so-called. the grey zone, i.e. increasing the share of unqualified seeds at the expense of certified seeds. The threat of equally important, and perhaps not perceived, is the possibility of influxing GMOs into Poland through illegal marketing of seeds. A well-designed law on the legal protection of plant varieties is an appropriate tool to improve the situation on the Polish seed market. The market sealing in conjunction with actions to stimulate the exchange of seeds by Polish farmers - through education and aid schemes - will have a positive impact on the economics of Polish farms, and the Polish plant breeding will provide an additional impetus for further development and will contribute to the creation of biological progress. Unfortunately, the proposed draft amendments to the Act do not meet the expectations of farmers and farmers. The proposed changes do not introduce systemic or comprehensive solutions. According to studies already carried out in the 1990s, the share of biological progress in production is more than 50%, whereas fertilization is only 20% and protection is 15%. . The Ministry of Agriculture itself considered that the reason for the low level of seed exchange in Poland is high fees for the use of material from a set of protected varieties. The Ministry cites examples of other countries, including Italy, where 85% the arable land area shall be sown annually with certified seed. In Poland it is only 18%. The solutions proposed in the Act will contribute to the decline in seed exchange as well as to the liquidation of Polish breeding. Therefore, the lack of comprehensive solutions related to seed breeding and replacement is a scandal. At this point, I would like to remind you of the recent situation in the case of SHiUZ. Polish breeders lost real control of the largest genetic company in the country. Today, SHiUZ is owned by French farmers, French farmers, and no one should be surprised if the station is primarily concerned with the interests of French farmers, not Polish farmers, who are a great competition for the French. The privatisation of SHiUZ is now an example of the inability of a country that cannot take care of the interests of its farmers. Therefore, we should make any changes in the area of seeding with exceptional caution, because this industry is already in foreign hands. Making plant production dependent on the size of crops, quality of cereals on foreign companies poses huge risks to our agriculture, exposes farmers to increase production costs and thus to the loss of competitiveness by Polish farms. Above all, however, this poses a threat to the food security of Poland. I believe that the solution is universality and at the same time very low level of fees. In conclusion, high fees, low level of breeding progress, a decrease in the profitability of farms and a reduction in the investment capacity of breeders will lead to the collapse of Polish breeding and then agriculture. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u343	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni wheels, I present my position towards the government's draft law amending the Law on the legal protection of plant varieties. The amendment concerns mainly the regulation of fees related to the use of seed. The solutions contained therein give farmers the freedom to decide whether to make the protected variety available free for their own reproductions for a specified period of time and introduce the possibility of collecting a single levy on own reproductions when buying certified seed. In addition, at the request of the Agricultural Chambers, the obligation whereby holders of agricultural land provided farmers with written information on the use of the harvest material was abolished. Today's project is an attempt to reach a compromise on the new rules for seed making, as the applicants point out, in response to farmers' demands that the current system of charging own reproductions has been extremely burdensome. As was evident from yesterday's meeting of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, it is essential for our agriculture to find the right solution in this regard. The replacement of seed is an important element of agricultural progress and was also indicated yesterday. Today, a small number of agricultural producers exchange seed. The compromise in this case is therefore definitely needed. However, there are many doubts as to whether the project presented will meet its objectives. Does the bill promote the exchange of seed and encourage whether farmers will be given too much freedom thanks to these regulations? Or are these solutions improving the situation only in the short term, and in the long run they will not pass the test? During the committee's work, it was proposed that the subcommittee set up for this purpose should bend to the proposed solutions. Although this proposal has been rejected, I believe that it is necessary to carry out further consultations and discussions on this project or to prepare a completely new amendment. This is because it is necessary to develop solutions which, as far as possible, will satisfy both sides of the conflict and whose effects will be long-term. Therefore, near Free and Solidarnia will not support this project.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-25-sejm-42-2.u345	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft amendment to the Law on specific rules for preparing for the implementation of flood-related investments contained in the parliamentary print project No. 1495 aims to apply flood management solutions to prepare investments in meteorological radars. The data obtained through the network of meteorological radars operated by the State's hydrological and meteorological service serve as a valuable source of information about the intensity of precipitation, which is the cause of local uplifts and submersions, as well as the creation of flood models used to determine and manage the best location of flood structures. The location of the meteorological radar shall be determined primarily by the quantity, quality and representativeness of the measurements carried out by that radar. The quality of the measurement results is influenced by: terrain, presence near trees, water reservoirs, buildings and other terrain obstacles, as measurements for meteorological purposes should be made as close as possible to the ground surface. The range of visibility for the lowest angles of raising the radar antenna is important. There are also important aspects such as the possibility of access, the possibility of connecting to ICT and energy infrastructure. Also an important factor is the incomplete coverage of local spatial planning of the country. All of these factors make the use of a specialty which radically shortens the administrative procedure for obtaining the necessary permits very reasonable in this case. High Izbo! The parliamentary project contained in print No 1495 entered the Sejm on 7 April this year. On 20 April, it was sent to the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry for first reading. On 11 May a first reading was held at the meeting of those joint committees, where the draft was sent to the Emergency Sub-Committee. The joint committees adopted the subcommittee report yesterday. Now the report of the joint committees, which I have the honour of presenting to the High House today, is in print No 1569. I move on to the presentation of the detailed solutions contained in this project. Art. 1 contains six changes. Amendment 1 refers to Article. 1 flood management and modifies the substantive scope proposed by the joint committees in the report. Recording, wording of the article. 1 is the following. First of all, the Act sets out specific rules and conditions for the preparation of investments in respect of: (a) flood prevention structures, (b) meteorological radar stations, hereinafter referred to as investments, and, secondly, the competent authorities in the matters referred to in point 1. This provision is the result of the work of the subcommittee and of the adoption of the proposed amendments. Amendment 2 refers to Article. It's a two-professional floodplain. Firstly, it modifies the definition of the investor by introducing the Institute of Meteorology and Water Management - the State Research Institute as one of the entities, and secondly, amending 2. introduces a new definition of meteorological radar stations. Amendment 3 refers to Article. 7 flood management and excludes the possibility of real estate trading covered by the application for an investment authorisation decision also in the case of the Institute of Meteorology and Water Management, as for other investors. Amendment 4 concerns Article. 9 point 5 of the mother's law. Lithium. a contains a provision that the decision to authorise the investment gives the possibility to identify the real estate which is part of the investment necessary for its implementation, owned or used forever by the Institute of Meteorology and Water Management. In turn in lithium. b refers to the real estate necessary for the operation of the investment, e.g. of real estate located in the protection zone which is neither owned nor in the perpetual use of the Institute of Meteorology and Water Management, for which the use of water is permanently restricted. Of course, this permanent limitation of use may in other cases be the basis for real estate owners to demand their redemption. And now, in these regulations that we are dealing with, which we are changing, for the owners of those properties for which the way of use is limited, there is a right to demand their redemption, even when the investor is the Institute of Meteorology and Water Management. This, in turn, concerns the amendment to Article 6. 1. She refers to the records of the article. Article 22 (2) (b) 2 of the mother's laws. High Izbo! Amendment 5 in turn concerns Article. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 of the mother's law and refers to the case where the Institute of Meteorology and Water Management is an investor and the investment is carried out on real estate owned by the Institute of Meteorology and Water Management or in its perpetual use and does not need to be taken over by the State Treasury or local government units. And that's all the changes - six - in Article. 1 of the draft law contained in the committee's report. Art. 2 of the draft contains a transitional provision providing that new provisions apply to cases initiated and not completed before the entry into force of the draft in question. Art. 3 provides that the law shall enter into force 14 days after the date of the announcement, i.e. this is the shortest decent of legal reasons for vacatio legis. High Izbo! The total project contained in print No 1569 of the combined infrastructure and environmental committees adopted 29 votes in favour, with one vote against and 11 abstentions. The joint committees in the course of the work on the project rejected two related amendments, amendment 2 and amendment 4 in Article. 1. amendments which go beyond the scope of the submission and concerning the definition of flood-related structures, but have nothing to do with meteorological radars, which are the subject of this amendment. These amendments, at the request of the applicants, are presented by the joint committees as two minority proposals to be voted on together. High Izbo! The public interest is that warning against hazardous atmospheric and hydrological phenomena should be based on reliable data. Our concern for our common security, also for the appropriate level of flood protection, is of the utmost importance, and therefore, on behalf of the Joint Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I ask that the Act amending the Law on specific rules for preparing for the implementation of the investments in flood prevention structures contained in print No 1569 be adopted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The sudden and very intense rainfall was a total surprise for the population living in areas where these phenomena occurred, but even more for the population living in neighbouring areas where there was no rainfall, but there was a devastating flood wave on small and calm watercourses. Today's technical measures allow us not to be passive observers and anticipate such situations. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, It is our duty to allow all modern measures to increase flood safety. In my view, the POLRAD network is an integral part of the modern flood safety system. There were allegations of her unconstitutionality. I remind you of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal on this law, the most recent, of 16 March 2017. Art. 1 paragraph 4 of the project - in accordance with the Constitution, Article. 2 laws - in accordance with the Constitution. The introduction of institutions for cyclical assembly is a new, additional way of defining a legal framework for the implementation of freedom of assembly - says the Constitutional Tribunal. This interference, which is proportionate, is constitutionally permitted, is laid down in the Act, serves to achieve certain values such as state security and public order. It can even be said that since the procedure for organising cyclical gatherings is more rigorous than those organized, so-called. notified, this granting of the temporary priority to these assemblies gives them this privileged character, and in fact compensates for the degree of interference of the legislator in the exercise of the freedom of these assemblies. The determination of the distance between two antagonistic assemblies per 100 m fully implements the right to express opinions both on the one and on the other, and the benefit of this is to increase the security of all: protesters, protesters and random passersby.
